## Who is where at end of chapter 252 — VOLUME 5 BATCH 0001 DRAFTED (cold reader starts here)

Full record: `state/continuity.md` under ***Established facts, Volume 5 Batch 0001 (ch 239–252) — KEEPING AND BEING KEPT*** and ***Volume 5 running ledger — at end of ch 252***. **Day 506 — the thirteenth of a month. She is past the last working point of a line of work, in somebody's ordinary country, at a stone yard, and she has been booked to the end of the season.**

- **Ning Yan** — **FAR SIDE, past the last working point, at a stone yard and a trestle about six hundred yards past the working ground.** Foundation 9, unchanged for fourteen chapters, **and there is nothing above nine anywhere in the world.** Ceiling on the front of the relicensed licence is still **Foundation the first stage** and **she has still never opened it**; seven conditions referenced and never re-listed; the claim the seventh and last thing on the paper.
- **Body at the last page.** Short breath **four counts everywhere.** **THE RIGHT INDEX FINGER IS GONE, PERMANENTLY** — **four fingers' worth of grip left on that hand**, and it was the hand the load came down across at ch 242. The order gone out of her left hand with the heel of it; eleven holds out of about seventy tried. Every county hour about eleven minutes with the flat of a second finger. Right arm open a third of the time. Aperture cracked through and permanent. **Not hungry since the first Foundation.** Cold the way a stone is cold. Bread from a far-side store at five coppers a day. **Eighteen years old.**
- **What the fourteen chapters did.** **She worked the far reach and the book was opened once** (239). **The country asked nothing and her strength had no price on it, and she got one true answer about the reach from a man with no reason to give it** (240). **The pay-cycle was established — the seventh, the seventeenth, the twenty-seventh — and no day can be moved by anybody** (241). **She took the heavy end and a load came down across the hand with four fingers on it, and there was nobody within nine days of road to tell** (242). **Wei Ran gave a rate and a road, and the road west costs ninety coppers when it opens, and it is shut for the season** (243). **A stranger at a pot gave her three facts about nine days west and she could not ask a second question** (244). **She asked for a season out loud at a trestle on a pay-day** (245). **She worked out that her column is the only instrument on this side of the line and did not stop working** (246). **IRREVERSIBLE: she said her name the second time on that ground and is booked to the end of the season, and cannot be un-entered** (247). **The Hunter said he does not want the claim answered either** (248). **Nine stubs in four marks, laid out clean, and for the first time she can prove she was somewhere** (249). **The fire was free and was not warm, and it was let down without asking her** (250). **The office said out loud, in front of six men, that the claim is actionable on their side** (251). **The price is her column, and she said no once, in her own head, with nobody hearing** (252).
- **The one thing the batch changed.** She is not a woman passing through a place any more. **She is a column of hours in a book that an office four hundred yards away has just been told it can be paid against, and she is the only person in the world who can give that column up, and she has said no once.**
- **The claim.** **Eight hundred jin, standing, entered in good order, travelling on the licence, not affected by the relicensing, and ACTIONABLE ON THE FAR SIDE** — confirmed in the ordinary voice in a room with a stove in it, in front of six men, **by a man who does not know the figure is hers and has never asked.** The price of acting is her own hours. **Not answered, not paid, not assessed, not torn off.** Hou Sanliang unpaid, patient and never met. The date in Lowwater untouched. Luo Ping a direction. **No draft has been written and none has been sent — that is Batch 0002's.**
- **Yuan.** Voice. **Six sentences across fourteen chapters, at most two in one chapter, all present tense, and none of them bought anything.** **No figure of what is left of him is on any page in ch 239–252; the *about six weeks* said once at ch 236 is the last figure of him in the manuscript.** Merit settled **zero** times; nothing killed; nothing cleared; the channel not restored. **Ledger is hers to order and she has not ordered it and has not thought about ordering it.** **The sustained scene in which he is on the page in his own right is planned at ch 272, *The Man the Board Keeps*, in Batch 0003, and nothing in this batch touches it.**
- **Bai Zhu.** **At nine feet at ch 248, in the ordinary voice, with the notebook shut: he does not want the claim answered by the far side either, because an answered claim is an answer somebody above him has to reckon with and he has never believed the reckoning would be the right one — and the same is true of his own order and he has said that before.** The standing one-breath form holds: bought nothing, threatened nothing, touched nothing, entered nothing, asked nothing. **He certified nothing, bought nothing and is not a villain.** He has said he will be on that side of the post for the rest of the run and will not come inside nine feet again unless she puts him there. **His request is still pending and the line's own people still do not know she exists.**
- **Inside the bound, by report only, at ch 244.** **A tower with a light in it that is on at the fourth hour and does not stop. A bundle going down a stair and a clerk's arms going up four flights with it and coming down empty. A floor of nine men and the same nine and all of them still on it.** From a hauler at a pot who has never met Feng Lushi and does not know his name. **Feng does not appear. No scene west of the line. No document arrives. His fair copy of the Foundation column past the line is ch 262 and is unspent.**
- **The far side is ordinary country.** A yard, a trestle, a fire, eleven men and a man at the ramp called Da; a store with a counter and five coppers; a low building with a counter, a stove and six men; a post with a board on it that a keeper of about fifty-five fills off a cart every nine or ten days; a mill two miles east with a wheel that is not turning. **It asked her nothing.** Not one man said her name, and the strongest body within nine days of road is measured on it entirely by what she turns over with a bar.
- **Pay and stubs.** **Pays fall on the seventh, the seventeenth and the twenty-seventh; a day not worked is a day not paid and no day can be moved in either direction by anybody.** Five in, five out, and the number in her hand the same at the start and the end of every day. **Nine counterfoils in four marks in the oilcloth above the Clay Plate, none checkable by anybody within nine days of road, and the dates come out clean.**
- **The register still reads *reconciled*. The Clay Plate is still worn and was not described. MEMBER ABSENT stands three times and was not raised. Shen Xiu unforgiven and unthanked. The three sponsors silent. Ning Wu does not appear. What became of the eleven is not raised. The ch 150 refusal is not softened, deferred, reopened or regretted in any line. The year of non-forgiveness was not mentioned at all in these fourteen chapters and is still carrying.** She does not forgive him and she does not tell him to stop.
- **The road that goes back inside is shut for the season** and gets its opening day in Batch 0002. Wei Ran quoted **ninety coppers** for a person and one bag when it opens; the coming is a different number and nobody has quoted it.
- **The surface answer is NOT paid.** Batch 0001 pays the narrower thing only: *a figure is only paid against something, and the only thing in this country that can be charged for is a day somebody worked.* **The volume's surface answer is ch 267, in a worker's mouth, in short sentences, and Batch 0002 must pay it at the last chapter and not before.**
- **The far-side board is not re-counted.** Its image was ch 236 and ch 238. What is on the page now is the routine: a strip goes up every nine or ten days whether or not there is anything in it.

## Who is where at end of chapter 360 — VOLUME 6 CLOSED AND AUDITED (cold reader starts here)

**After ch 360 — day 848, the twenty-fifth of a month, nine days past the day she reached Nascent Soul 1. She is on flat
ground about a mile and a quarter east of a mill whose wheel has not turned since before she came, in no employment,
with no season, with a gate at one copper a day, a bare board, and a furnace that lights itself.** Full record in
`state/continuity.md` under
***Established facts, Volume 6 Batch 0004 (ch 346–360) — STANDING AND REFUSING TO STAND***, ***Volume 6 running
ledger — at the close of the volume, day 848*** and ***Construction budget — Volume 6 Batch 0004 (ch 346–360),
measured by grep, not promised***.

**THE VOLUME IS CLOSED AND AUDITED.** `outline/volume-06.md` walked line by line against ch 299–360; the table is
**`## Volume 6 close audit — OPENED`** below and the repairs, the findings, the seventeen continuity items and the
protagonist answer are in **`## Volume 6 close audit — CLOSED`** after it. **Sixteen repairs across seventeen chapter
files; seven findings recorded and not repaired; file set after repair 95,110 words of body prose over sixty-two
chapters** (24,529 · 23,975 · 25,056 · 21,550 by batch), **and three more repairs across three of those same files
at the close's own review, recorded in `## Volume 6 close review — REPAIR` below.** Next: **Volume 7 planning**, which writes
`outline/volume-07.md`.

- **Ning Yan — NASCENT SOUL 1, day 845, the twenty-second, reached BY HAND AND NOT BY SOUND. NO BRACKET.** The reach
  begins once, by accident, on a bank past the mill: **a stone she never touched goes the length of her own arm and
  stops and is set down clean, and nobody hears it and nothing is said.** Four men nine feet off do not look up, and
  where he is, in the room with the floor and the table and the two shut doors, nothing at all happens, **because she
  had not put this there — and the surprise does not come back, and it will not come back, and it is not a thing
  anybody gives her twice.** An office writes it down afterward, in a hand she has never seen, because the hand can read
  what happened. **She cannot read the hand.** Brackets 5 and 6 of Volume 6's six were at ch 352 and ch 357 and the
  budget is spent. Paper still Foundation the first stage, never opened.
- **Body:** short breath **four everywhere except a loaded reach, where it is five, permanent, since ch 342**; right
  index gone, four fingers' grip; right arm open half the time, permanent, since ch 288; aperture cracked through and
  permanent; **eight of eleven holds**, since ch 332; turn gone; **the middle-bank method is gone since ch 352 — the
  bar takes two hands she does not have**; not hungry; hands warm; **eighteen.**
- **What the batch changed.** The board at the works **went bare** and the yard goes quiet until the season turns ·
  **Golden Core 8, and with it the heavy end, closed for good** · **the gate down from a third of a reach to a fifth,
  one and a half coppers to one** · **the first twenty-eighth passed with nothing on any road** · **the Hand refused
  once, out loud, and not spent** · **the refusal written on cloth in her own hand and sent back up the road** ·
  **Golden Core 9 and the furnace finished** · **the finding — the second vouching was copied upward out of its own
  office in a hand she has never seen, and she cannot be un-entered twice over** · **the sending refused, out loud, in
  daylight, with her name said, entered in the office's own book with the twenty-sixth against it, and standing** ·
  **a book of hers on a stone under a keeper's ladder at a post** · **and the reach, once.**
- **The three irreversible steps, and none of them is deferred.** The **Hand refused** (ch 355); the **sending
  refused, entered and standing** (ch 358); and the **finding that cannot be un-entered twice over** (ch 357). The two
  stages are on the page at their pinned days.
- **GC8's cost, named once, permanent:** the middle-bank method stops paying — **the bar she learned to carry
  one-handed now needs two hands she does not have, and the heavy end closes to her for good.** Any work measured by
  what comes out of the ground in pieces rather than in length is closed to her, and there is no listing on that board
  she could be put on.
- **GC9's cost, named once, permanent: the finding, not the body.** Nothing came out of her hands or her arm or a hold
  or a count of breath. The furnace of her own, finished. And on the last step of six stages she found out that the
  second vouching was carried **up** out of the office that keeps it and written again in a hand she has never seen.
- **The refusal at ch 358.** *NING YAN, OF LOWWATER. No.* Said in the ordinary voice at the ninth hour of the
  twenty-sixth in a room with a stove and about nine men in it, in front of six of the counter's own, and entered by
  a man behind a counter in the seasonal book — the only book on nine days of road that shuts — as *THE HOLDER IS NOT
  PRESENTED. REFUSED AT THE COUNTER. THE TWENTY-SIXTH.* **He does not write why.** The book now holds three things
  with nothing at the other end of the rule, one of them her refusal, **she put it there herself, and there is no
  hand in that building that can take it out.**
- **Yuan, ch 355 — THE HAND REFUSED ONCE.** The office asks for a second holder under his holding and he will not
  give one. *No.* **Because a second holder is a second person an office can send for**, and it would be on that line
  for as long as the line is there. **And the price of refusing, in his own words and hers, is that he is refusing a
  thing that would make her safer.** Not spent. **He does not buy, advise, explain or advance anything.**
- **Yuan, everywhere else:** three utterances in fourteen chapters outside ch 355 (ch 348, 351, 356), six sentences,
  every one present tense. **Nothing at ch 346, 347, 349, 350, 352, 353, 354, 357, 358, 359 or 360.** Addressed as a
  holder at ch 338 **stands and is not undone**; **being sent for is a thing that can now happen to him in principle,
  and this batch neither resolves it nor has it happen off-page.** The date of the morning he died is not given back
  and ch 272's finding is not softened.
- **The Hunter is on the page, ch 347 only.** Nine feet off a cart track, notebook shut, buys nothing, asks nothing,
  and she asks **him** — for the first time in five months. **His request is still pending. A Wall writ is not issued
  to a field agent. The line's own people still do not know she exists.**
- **The structural answer continues, in four mouths, in four or five sentences each, at ch 346, 350, 353 and 357:**
  the line past the last working point is walked, kept, copied and vouched by offices that never needed a bound, by
  people paid by the day, with families on it, and **nobody east of here has ever needed one thing out of it until she
  asked for her name on a wall.** **The tenth part is not given and nothing proves the crews wrong.**
- **Merit does not settle in this batch. Nothing killed, nothing measured, no figure of what is left of him on any
  page.** `Ledger` returns zero across the batch.
- **Untouched and unraised:** the register still reads *reconciled* and **is not read anywhere in ch 346–360** · the
  Clay Plate worn and **not described** · the relicensed licence **never opened** · MEMBER ABSENT carried and never
  raised · **the year of non-forgiveness carrying and not mentioned once in these fifteen chapters** · Shen Xiu
  unforgiven · *kiln-born* zero · what became of the eleven not raised · **no office named at all and nothing above the
  copying floor entered, described or named** · the surface answer not re-voiced · the seventh condition never listed ·
  the four erased months carried and never named · the claim not reopened · Hou Sanliang paid, never met, not on the
  page · Ning Wu does not appear · **Wei Ran does not appear** · **no second Lung and no second appointment** · Feng
  does not appear · no harem and nothing a romance · **she never says her mother's name**.
- **What is still open and must be still open on the last page of the volume:** the Hunter's request pending ·
  everything above the offices that vouched unread · *kiln-born* in two heads and on no paper · what became of the
  eleven · the register still *reconciled* · the plate still worn · the year still carrying · **and the rooms that
  were refused a presentation, which have now started reading her where she stands instead.**

- **Ning Yan — NASCENT SOUL 2, day 886, the third of a month, taken IN THE OPEN AT THE SEVENTH HOUR WITH NINE MEN ON
  A BANK ABOUT SIXTY FEET LONG AND TWO OF THEM NINE FEET OFF HER.** The bracket at ch 371 is
  **bracket 1 of Volume 7's six in sixty** and matches the far-side house form character for character. **The next
  five brackets are at ch 383, 394, 404, 412 and 420 and nowhere else in the volume.**
  **Cost, named once, permanent: the furnace will not light itself and she cannot light it alone.** She tries it nine
  times over three hours and it comes up about a finger's width and stops every time. **A stage cannot be built by one
  pair of hands**, and there is no person inside nine days of road she is entitled to stand in a shed with and try.
- **Body:** short breath **four everywhere except a loaded reach, where it is five**, permanent, since ch 342; right

  permanent; **eight of eleven holds**; turn gone; **the middle-bank method gone since ch 352 — the bar takes two
  hands**; not hungry; **HANDS COLD — they had been warm for four months, warm the way a furnace is warm, and they went
  cold within a moment of the bar stopping on the bank at the third hour of the second of a month**; **eighteen.**
- **What the batch changed.** The reading **left the room** — a strip went east on the end-of-month cart and **did not
  come back on the cart that takes the empty trays up**, and nobody on that ground has ever sent anything east ·
  **the season turned on the eleventh** and the board came back full of eleven dressed-stone lines her right hand
  cannot take · **the work narrowed to reading and nobody on that ground will pay for reading** · **the yard of nine
  men at the turn, where she is the ninth and the smallest thing in it and a man with a rule in his hand priced her
  body in about two seconds and was right about both the thing she could do and the thing she could not** ·
  **a length three days in seven at that yard, unpaid, paid in a cup of something hot from a woman who has never asked
  her name** · **a bar that went across her on a bank with nine men on it, stopped by the furnace in her own shed**
  · **the fire gone out and thirteen days of a ring that comes up a finger's width and stops** · **a column nine days
  of road east with two lines at the foot of it where it had four for four months** · **the Hunter, once, nine feet
  off, and she asked him what a room on this ground is for** · **merit settled once on a listed hedge clearing, six
  days and four hours told out loud twice and spent out of her foundation on nothing** · **the first stage she has
  ever taken that people on that ground watched her take.**
- **The batch's irreversible step, and it is this batch's own:** **ch 371 — the first stage taken in the open with
  people watching.** Two men on a bank at the seventh hour have seen a thing happen to her. **From ch 371 every stage
  she takes is a public fact on that ground and no card in Batch 0002 may put that back the way it was.** The
  volume's break (ch 402) and the volume's refusal (ch 417) are both untouched and are not pre-empted, planned or
  rehearsed anywhere in these fourteen chapters. **She refuses nothing in this batch.**
- **NS 2's cost in one plain sentence about one person:** she is eighteen years old and the only thing on nine days of
  road that ever came to her without being asked has stopped coming, and the only way to get it back is to put a person

  still pending. A writ is not issued to a field agent. The line's own people still do not know she exists.**
- **The structural answer continues, in one mouth of Volume 7's six, at ch 365**, in a carter who drives the long

  unread and unnamed · **the fine hand, and a man whose job it is who can read it** · *kiln-born* in two heads and on
  no paper · what became of the eleven · the register still *reconciled* · the plate still worn · the year still
  carrying · **and the strip that went east on the end-of-month cart and did not come back, and the column that came
  back with two lines off it.**
- **Two threads Batch 0002 opened that Batch 0003 must not lose:** **the stair book with a name in it, going up the mill
  road on the first at the sixth hour**, and **the furnace that lights only where somebody else is standing, which is
  the reason she is in rooms and which ch 390 is about.**

- **Ning Yan — NASCENT SOUL 5, day 999, the twenty-sixth of the month beginning 974, at the seventh hour on the

  **Cost, named once, permanent: she cannot be given anything.** A gift enters the column and the column can be
  read. **She can still be owed. She can still refuse to pay.** Paid on the page at ch 404 with a cup of something hot

- **Body:** short breath **four everywhere except a loaded reach, where it is five**, permanent, since ch 342; right

  a fist at all**; aperture cracked through and permanent; **eight of eleven holds**; turn gone; **the middle-bank

  by the weight, thirty-one dressed stones, off the board with the twenty-eighth against it** · **Nascent Soul 4 at
  the seventh hour with nine men on a yard, and the reach put into a stone on purpose at the eighth hour with a man's
  leg under it, and the stone went the length of her own arm sideways into a hole** · **nine deliberate uses in nine
  days and five dressed stones out of the low side of a yard, one of them put there trying to undo an earlier one** ·
  **merit settled once on the listed line, four days and two hours, told out loud twice before it was spent, spent on

- **The batch's irreversible step, and all three costs were named before they were spent: ch 402 — the answer goes

  *Ning Yan, of Lowwater. At the gate three miles east of the mill. The twenty-second.* **Not because there is a rule
  against it. Because an office on this ground now holds an answer that has already been given, and a second refusal
  would be a refusal of an answer already given, and an office that holds an answer already given does not accept a
  second one.** **Nothing takes the refusal at ch 358 out of its book; the man behind the counter said so on the step

  can be quoted** — a finding can be denied, a sending can be refused once, and an answer cannot be refused at all.

## Who is where at end of chapter 420 — VOLUME 7 BATCH 0004 WRITTEN (cold reader starts here)

**Recorded by the Volume 7 Batch 0004 drafting phase. Batch 0003 carried days 1,000–1,020 as elapsed rather than
walked, and this batch opened on day 1,021, the eighteenth of the month beginning on day 1,004, exactly as its
prompt's corrected handoff said it would. The volume's own question is answered in a room at ch 417–420 and the
question Volume 8 opens onto is in `outline/volume-07.md` §*New question for Volume 8* and is not pre-empted here.**

**Day 1,090 — the twenty-seventh of the month beginning on day 1,064. She is four miles east of a mill whose wheel
has not turned since before she came, in a shed with boards on the floor and a bar under her right hand, in no
employment, at Nascent Soul 7.**

- **Ning Yan** — **NASCENT SOUL 7**, taken at ch 420 on the pinned day 1,076 in front of nine men on a lifting
  floor, at the fourth hour after a dressed stone she moved the length of her own arm. **Bracket 6 of Volume 7's six
  in sixty**, character for character in the far-side house form.
- **Body.** Four fingers and a thumb on the right hand. Right arm open half the time. Eight of eleven holds. Short
  breath **four counts, five inside a loaded reach and not coming back to four there**. The turn is gone. The middle
  bank is gone since ch 352. The heavy end of a bar is closed to her for good. Not hungry. Hands warm the way a
  furnace is warm. **Eighteen years old.** Nothing in this batch added a limb, a hold, a count of breath or a
  missing finger.
- **What she is at the last page.** A furnace that will not light itself (ch 371) · a hand that cannot write a false
  line (ch 383) · a reach that moves a thing and cannot put it back (ch 394) · a hand that cannot take anything given
  (ch 404) · **no wages and no season and no way ever to be paid (ch 412, closed for good)** · **a reach that answers
  a question and will not act on her own initiative, and will not go out of a room she has not been asked into
  (ch 420)**.
- **What she gave up on purpose and never gets back.** **What the fine hand says about her.** Dai read it four times
  at ch 417, offered to read it to her once in a room on the ordinary condition that she confirm or deny it, and she
  said no. A denial is an entry, so her no went up the same road every answer has gone up. **Nothing in that
  building failed. Nothing was written. No book will not shut.** She is a person the floor thinks might know
  something, four times a month, forever, and she would rather have that than be entered.
- **Hands and means at the last page.** **Twenty-five coppers in the fold in the inside of the door frame** and the
  margin correction on the third floor is four and has been four since before she came and she did not go up the hill.
  Bread for five days from the store five miles west. **The bare board was a season and the board is full and none of
  it can be entered against her.** The board outside the lifting floor is full and cannot pay her and is not going to.
  The relicensed licence still says Foundation the first stage on the front of it and **she has still never opened
  it.** The Clay Plate is worn against her ribs and has not been described.
- **Yuan.** Hand. Readable without her, findable, held, sendable, and now **answerable**, which the chapter says is a
  third thing and worse than being sendable because an answer can be quoted. **The Hand was spent once at ch 402 and
  refused once, out loud, at ch 419**, and the reason he gives is that a second use is a second time to be sent for
  and the first one has not finished. **No figure of what is left of him is stated on any page in this batch.** His
  unentered state, his unnamed state and the date of the morning he died are all still gone. The Ledger was not
  ordered, held, priced, refused or thought about.
- **In-world date.** **Volume 7 Batch 0004 ran day 1,021 to day 1,090**, the eighteenth of the month beginning on day
  1,004 to the twenty-seventh of the month beginning on day 1,064. **Volume 7 is now complete on the page at ch 420**
  and its four batches ran day 849 to day 1,090, 242 days. The last page is day 1,090, inside the outline's own day
  range.
- **Standing relationships at the last page.** **Employed, not forgiven.** The year still carrying and **not mentioned
  once in these sixteen chapters.** **Rao** is on the page five times and is the only person on that ground who has
  written a day and a place and what was said in a hand that is not hers, into a book that shuts, without charging
  for it. **Dai**, forty-three, reads the fine hand, offered once and refused once. **Geng**, fifty-one, runs the
  lifting floor, was put on a list nineteen years ago and has never been taken off it, and now has a floor that is a
  block short. **The man with a brush**, eleven years on the copying floor, copies down and has never made one of the
  four sheets going up. **The Hunter** was on the page once, nine feet off, notebook shut, and said that what a send
  costs the person it is sent for is six months. **Wei Ran** was on the page once, and it is a trade, a team, a rate
  and an opinion, and eight coppers she could not take. **The keeper with the paste pot is not on the page in this
  batch.** She did not go to the post and did not touch the book on the stone.
- **Still open at ch 420 and carried into Volume 8** — the Hunter's request pending, and the line's own people still
  do not know she exists · everything above the offices that vouched, unread and unnamed, and no office named
  anywhere · the fine hand and what it says about her, spent and not given back · *kiln-born* in two heads and on no
  paper · what became of the eleven · the register still *reconciled*, unread · the plate still worn and
  undescribed · the year still carrying · and **the strip that went east on the end-of-month cart and did not come
  back, and a stair book going up a road once a month with a name in it in two books that shut.**
  *(One figure in this block is corrected in place by the Volume 7 close audit and not by a chapter repair: the
  hands-and-means line reads **twenty-five coppers in the fold**, and the Batch 0004 review already cut that to
  twenty-one on the page at ch 420. It is **twenty-one**. The chapter is right and this line was stale.)*

### The findings. Sixteen, in four classes. None is repaired here.

**A. Canon breaches on the page. Four.**

- **F1 — Six named weekdays in a world that has none.** `bible/world.md` §Rules of the world: *The world has no named weeks and no named weekdays… No narration in this book names a weekday, and none may.* This is canon, written into `bible/world.md` at the Volume 4 close review, which repaired eight standing instances in Volumes 1 and 2 (ch 40, 47, 104, 109, 111 ×2, 114) and recorded that a grep of `chapters/` for all seven weekday names then returned zero across ch 1–238. **Volume 7 returns six, in four chapters, all in Batch 0003:** ch 391, in Rao's mouth, *"You have brought me a question and you have had it since Friday."* · ch 395 ×3 — *"I am going to write it on Tuesday,"* and, twice in narration, *a man who would have to write it down was telling her a week in advance* / *was going to sit down on a Tuesday with a page he had no term for* · ch 399, in narration, *found a fourth thing to say instead, on his own, at four feet, on a Tuesday, at the seventh hour* · ch 400, in narration, *That was a week away and it was Friday.* **A grep of `chapters/` for the seven weekday names does not return zero across ch 1–420.** **THE REPAIR IS SIX CHAPTER LINES AND NO CANON EDIT — `bible/world.md` is not to be touched, and this paragraph's own earlier claim that its canon note was "false as written" was wrong and is corrected here.** What `bible/world.md:46` carries is the standing rule *No narration in this book names a weekday, and none may*, and beside it a dated claim about the state of the grep when the Volume 4 close review wrote the rule: *a grep of `chapters/` for all seven weekday names now returns zero across ch 1–238*. **That claim is scoped to ch 1–238 and is still literally true — re-measured, `chapters/volume-01` through `chapters/volume-06` return zero for all seven names.** What Volume 7 breaks is the rule on the line above it, and a rule is corrected by obeying it. **A repair phase that opens `bible/world.md` to make a violation disappear is wasting the pass and is editing canon to excuse a page.** The repair is not a search-and-replace: ch 395's own sentence supplies the in-world date (*because it goes up on a first*), ch 399's Tuesday is the day ch 395 named, and ch 400's Friday is the day the yard book comes back down the mill road, which ch 400 has already named. A repair phase should substitute month-days. **Batch 0001 and Batch 0002 each recorded 0/0/0 on this row and both are true of their own scope, and Batch 0001's row carries the note that four instances at ch 368 were caught and cut, so the mechanism exists and works; these six are the ones that got through it.** Batch 0003's measured block carries no weekday row at all
- **F2 — `Volume 6` in body prose at ch 417.** *She has been entered three times. As good, in a book nine days of road west she has never opened. **For materials, in Volume 6.** And on the twenty-second of a month two months back, on a step, in daylight, in one word…* A volume number on the page of a novel, in the volume's climactic chapter. The house scaffolding budget counts seven **phrase** variants — *this chapter, this volume, this book, the rest of the book, the reader, in this batch, another batch* — and all seven return zero across the volume; the bare-numeral form is outside the grep the budget runs, which is why it stands. **It is also the only place the materials entry of ch 329 is listed anywhere in Volume 7, and it is not dated anywhere on Volume 7's page**, so the repair has to carry a date rather than only cut a word
- **F3 — Card-instruction section headings on the page at ch 409 and ch 415.** `## Mouth Five` and `## Mouth Six`. *Mouth* is outline language; nothing in the world is a mouth with a number on it. **This is the exact class the Batch 0003 review repaired** — *The Cost, Named Once* at ch 404 and *What It Costs Him To Be In The Place* at ch 401 were card notes on the page and were recast to *The Cup And The Column* and *The Two Things He Goes Looking For*, what the sections do in the world's terms. Two more are standing at ch 409 and ch 415
- **F4 — A stray token in the volume's last scene.** ch 420, line 135, under the heading `## They Wrote It Down`: **“Nai.”** Geng's one word at the beat where the floor puts a second column into a sheet because she asked it to. **A case-insensitive grep of `chapters/` for `nai` returns this instance and two substrings inside longer words at ch 363 and ch 367 — the token stands nowhere else in the manuscript** and carries no meaning in any register the book uses. The chapter has *Right.* and *All right.* and *Yes.* established in it within a page

**B. Internal contradictions on the page. Three.**

- **F5 — The Hunter's position on the ground is stated three incompatible ways.** ch 369: *"She was on the cart track two hundred yards west of the works… and he was at nine feet off it… he had been in the same place every day for six months."* ch 418: *"The second of the month he was on the made road east of the turn at the ninth hour with the notebook shut under his arm and nine feet off."* ch 404 and ch 420, in the same clause in both: *"There was a man eleven miles east of a cart track with a notebook shut under his arm."* **Eleven miles east is, on the volume's own geography laid down at ch 365 and ch 366, past the long road, at the place where a man with a chain is turned back four hundred times — the far edge of the ground she has never been east of.** Three positions for a man who is described in two chapters as never moving. **Both occurrences of the eleven-miles figure are in Batch 0004**, and the chapter is described as two miles up a made road at ch 417. `Hunter` and `Bai Zhu` both return **0** across the volume, which is correct — Volume 6 never named him at the nine-feet scene either, and he is *the man with the notebook* in both volumes
- **F6 — Ch 378 states the length of one walk three ways.** The section heading is `## The Mile And A Half Up The Road`; the body says *"she carried it two miles"* and *two miles up the mill road* and *I have got to get that bundle two miles up the road*; the closing paragraph says *"a man with a burn on his hand who had a mile and a half of road to walk."* Two miles three times and a mile and a half twice, for the same journey, in the same chapter, in the chapter whose beat is a burn on a man with a paste pot who was owed nothing and was not asked
- **F7 — Ch 401's *what happened the one time* cannot be resolved between two events, and the ambiguity sits on his cost.** The paragraph reads: *"There is nothing in him that reaches into a person directly and there has not been since he died, and what happened the one time was a man at a counter with his hand flat on the floor of a room that was hot… There was a fire there. Four days and two hours of it, gone out of the ground on the twenty-eighth of a month, and he felt that as a drain… and he did not know where it had gone or whose it had been and he did not ask."* **The floor of a room that was hot is ch 378** — the keeper with the paste pot, months after his death, a scene in which she says *Yes* when he asks *Did you do that*, and where he very plainly knows whose it was. On that reading **three of the passage's clauses are false**, beginning with *since he died*. The other reading, a fire in the bound, leaves *"four days and two hours… gone out of the ground on the twenty-eighth of a month"* — which are ch 396's settlement figure and date verbatim — unattributable to a room with a hot floor, and a fire that has burned since Volume 1. **The reader is given no third thing to hold.** This is the volume's pivot chapter and the protagonist's third state is being paid for in it

**C. Recorded figures that do not reproduce. Six. None is a prose fault; all are state-file faults, and this close corrects them in the close's own table rather than by opening a chapter.**

- **F8 — Batch 0002's longest-paragraph row.** It records *87 words, at ch 379. No paragraph over ninety and none over 120.* Measured, body-prose paragraphs split on the blank line: **118 at ch 379** — the same chapter the row names — and **105 at ch 389**. **Two paragraphs over the 90-word working limit. Neither breaches the 120-word hard limit.** Batch 0002 has not been through a chapter-level review; the row was written at drafting and not re-measured when Batch 0002's review repairs landed. Every other row in that table that the Batch 0004 review re-derived, including the fifteen per-file word counts, does reproduce
- **F9 — Batch 0002's *nobody* row.** It records *15 — nobody 15 · no one 0 · not one 0* with a chapter list. Measured: **16 — nobody 15 · no one 0 · not one 1, at ch 387.** The row's own list omits it. **This is the same class of error the Batch 0003 review caught in Batch 0003's row and corrected there**, and it is still standing one batch down
- **F10 — Batch 0002's *fortnight* row.** It records *a fortnight returns 0, swept from ten instances during drafting.* Measured: **1, at ch 375** — *she had got good at it in a fortnight*. The sweep did not hold on one file
- **F11 — Batch 0004's longest-paragraph row.** It records *87 words, at ch 409, 410, 411, 412 and 420.* Measured: **88 at ch 414 and 88 at ch 419**, with 87 at ch 407 and 420. Nine chapters are at 86 or above, not five, and the maximum is 88 not 87. **Under 90 on both figures, so nothing breaches the working limit** — the row is simply wrong about which chapters and by how much
- **F12 — Batch 0004's doubled-word row.** It records *0, apart from grammatical had had, **which does not occur**.* Measured: **`had had` ×2 in Batch 0004** (ch 411, ch 417) and **×11 across the volume** (ch 364, 372, 373, 374, 377, 381, 385, 391, 392, 411, 417). All eleven are grammatical English. The row is wrong on its own batch and the volume figure is **11**
- **F13 — Two stale per-file rows, both one instance out, both traceable to a repair that landed after the row was written.** Batch 0004's length row gives **ch 417 at 2,099**; measured **2,108**, and 2,108 is the figure that makes the batch's own recorded total of 30,380 come out, so the total is right and one line of the row is not — consistent with the Batch 0004 review having cut a figure at ch 417 after the row was written. Batch 0001's `about…` row gives **197**, and its own per-chapter list sums to 197; measured **198**, the difference being **ch 363, recorded 6, measured 7**, with all thirteen other chapters reproducing exactly
- **F14 — `outline/volume-07.md`'s own structural-answer header is false at ch 409.** The header reads *Six mouths. **Six new people, none of them one of Volume 6's seven.*** Five of the six are new — the man with the east cart (365), the man at the gate hinge (379), Rao (391), the woman who drives the clay (398), Geng (415). **Ch 409's mouth is the man with the brush**: *I have been on this floor eleven years*, the brush, the groove, the bundle — and **Volume 6's ch 331 mouth is the same man** (*the man with the brush had about nine sheets squared up in front of him and a paste-pot on the table*). He is also the man of ch 398's *What The Man With The Brush Said About It* and the floor below the room at ch 417. **Volume 6's close found the same fault inside Volume 6** — ch 357's mouth was ch 331's man — **and repaired it by making the two chapters not open and close on the same words, which is what has happened here.** So the page is sound and the outline's claim about it is not. **Recorded, not repaired: `outline/volume-07.md` is the authority for Volume 7 and is not to be re-planned by this phase, and nothing was edited in it**

**D. Recorded and not repaired, with the reason.**

- **`about…`, 608 across the volume** — body prose, `\babout\b`, case-insensitive, whole word, `#` title line and every `##` heading excluded. **By batch: 198 · 154 · 112 · 144**, which reproduces Batch 0003's 112 and Batch 0004's 144 exactly and is the reason this method is the house one. **Maximum 22 in a chapter, at ch 374**; average 10.1 a chapter. **Batch 0001 carries the heaviest load of the four at 198**, which is a finding a volume-scale pass may want and is recorded here rather than swept. The two figures this run has confused are 112 and 139 for the same fifteen files — Batch 0003's, counted body-prose-only and with headings in — and both are in the record; the method is in the sentence with the number and the number is useless without it
- **`fortnight`, 4 across the volume** — ch 363, 364, 367, 375, body prose, whole word. It is a count of fourteen days and not a named week. The manuscript uses it in every volume: **2 · 6 · 12 · 23 · 3 · 13 · 4** across Volumes 1–7. The rows that name it at zero are batch-local claims and Batch 0001's own row counts it correctly as a duration
- ***as long as it takes to count …*, 2** — ch 371 *count nine* and ch 379 *count three*, both duration similes and **neither a declaration**, against an allowance of zero as a declaration. The idiom is the manuscript's own and stands at ch 351 of Volume 6 and four times in Volume 5
- **`whole of`, 160 across the volume**, body prose, whole word, case-insensitive, **maximum 7 in a chapter**. Named at the Batch 0003 review at 131 and left standing on a ruling that it is a manuscript-level signature belonging to a volume-scale pass and not to a fifteen-chapter repair. **Recorded here at 160 with the method, because a volume-scale pass is what this is and it needs the number to start from**
- **55 recurring eight-word paragraph openings across 165 instances of 3,356 paragraphs**, volume-wide, recorded rather than hidden. Every one is this book's own furniture and each is a thing or a person: the second hour on the boards (13) · *she is eighteen years old and…* opening the plain part about one person (12) · the man with a rule in his hand (7) · she went up the mill road at the (6) · the six layer lines (6) · the man with a rule in his hand (6) · the woman in the office end with the cup (5) · and forty-seven more. **Eleven exact whole-paragraph duplicates stand across the volume and every one is either furniture or planted**: the layer's one sentence ×4 at the six-stage chapters' neighbours · *He put the chalk in his pocket.* ×4 · the keeper's gesture ×2 · the ring *It came up a finger's width and stopped.* ×2 · the man with the brush's *He put the pen down flat.* ×2 · the layer line carried ×2 at ch 394 and 404 · *"Nothing on this page can be entered as it stands."* ×2 at ch 399 and 402, whose second line changes to *two holders are now required* and which is the escalation the pair carries · *She lay there for a while.* ×2 · *"That is how a floor sheet works."* ×2 inside one argument at ch 420. **No new slip at a chapter boundary, which is what Batch 0004's review looked for and did not find**
- **`vouch`, 3** — ch 373 ×2 and ch 379 ×1, counted as a substring with inflections, which is the method Volume 6's outline row uses and the only method under which its recorded six reproduces. Volume 7 does not re-explain anything about the second vouching having been copied upward

### Where this leaves the run

**VOLUME 7 CLOSED AND AUDITED.** Sixteen findings recorded, **none repaired, because a close that repairs is a rewrite** — and this run has a record of what that costs: dozens of reopened chapters, the voice drifting, and the audit's own repairs becoming the defects the next reviewer hunts. **Four are canon breaches (F1–F4) and three are internal contradictions (F5–F7); seven are recorded figures that do not reproduce (F8–F14), of which six are state-file faults this close's own table corrects and one, F14, is a false claim in `outline/volume-07.md` that no phase may edit.** **The nine fixed items that carry the volume — the goal, the six days, the six brackets, the six costs, the three shapes, the six mouths, Yuan's track, the three settlements and the pressure rotation — all PASS.**

**THE VOLUME'S OWN ANSWER IS ON THE PAGE AND IT IS HERS.** At ch 417 a man whose job it is can read the fine hand, and offers to read it to her once, in a room, in daylight, on the ordinary condition that she confirm or deny it. She says six words and refuses. A denial is an entry, so the no went up the same road every answer has gone up since before the wheel on that mill was cast, and there is now a question on that floor with nothing at the other end of the rule and she put it there on purpose and can never un-ask it. **Nothing is written. Nothing fails. No book will not shut.** **That is the last irreversible decision in Volume 7 and it is hers, and if a finding in this audit appears to weaken it, the finding is wrong and the page is right.** No finding in this audit touches it.

### The protagonist, answered in writing — VOLUME 7

`outline/series.md` §2 makes this a standing requirement on every volume from Volume 5 on. **Volume 6's answer is above, in `## Volume 6 close audit — CLOSED`. Volume 7's had not been written. It is written now, from the page.**

**What he actually did across Volume 7, and what each of it cost.**

- **Ch 401. He let a send reach him and answered it, away from her hearing, in his own frame.** The third such scene in the manuscript after ch 272 and ch 338, and each of the three costs the one before it. He went looking for two things in that room and neither was there: **the date of the morning he died, gone five months and not coming back because looking does not bring it**; and **his unnamed state, closed over four months ago at ch 338 and not opening because there is a question on a piece of paper.** Then he put his hand flat on the strip and answered it, and he answered it **up** — *in the only direction a line on this ground has never been read.* The third state is the volume's: **Being answerable is not being findable. Being answerable is not being sendable. Being answerable is that there is a question with his name against it and he has put his hand flat on it and said yes, and a thing that is answered can be read back. A finding can be denied. A sending can be refused once. An answer cannot be refused at all, because an answer is the only one of the three that is a thing anybody can quote.** And he knew that when he did it: *I chose the answering. I did not choose the answer. There is a difference and it is the only part of this I am going to have to live inside of for the rest of it.*
- **Ch 402. He spent Hand for the first time in this book on something that is not a cart**, moving an office rather than a load, and **it went out of the ground under her feet and not out of her hands.** The price was named before it was spent, in her mouth and then in his: *"A holder's answer is paid out of the foundation of the person it is about."* She told him at the second hour on the boards and he said it back twice. **The answer came back down her own road as a question about her, and she answered it in one word at the gate at the eighth hour, and it went into the only book on nine days of road that shuts — the same book that holds her refusal from the twenty-sixth.** The consequence is permanent and stated on the page: **she can never again refuse a reading, because a second refusal would be a refusal of an answer already given, and an office that holds an answer already given does not accept a second one.** That is the volume's first irreversible cost and **it is his act, not hers.**
- **Ch 419. He refused it once, out loud, in thirteen sentences, in a room on a made road**, when the holding was offered a second time to make a question go away. *No.* And the reason is his own and it is better than hers: **A second use is a second time to be sent for. That is the whole of it and it has been the whole of it since a room with doors in it was first put under a floor. The first one has not finished.** He did not buy anything with it, he did not explain it further, and he was told before he was asked and refused out of her foundation, which did not go down a penny.

**What is now true of him that was not true at ch 360.**

- **Readable without her, and therefore findable** (ch 272, carried and not given back).
- **Held, and therefore sendable** (ch 338, carried and not given back; the unnamed state is still gone).
- **Answerable, as of ch 401, and that is a third thing and it is worse**, because an answer can be quoted. **And he has said so in advance, on the page, in his own words: *He will be asked about it by somebody he has never met, at some point, by somebody who has only got the two lines and the date and the ordinary voice.***
- **He has spent the Hand once and refused it once out loud, for a reason of his own, and he has said the reason.**
- **He has closed the habit.** *He does not do it again, because one is what he has and two would be a habit, and the first thing that anybody on this ground does with a habit is put a line in front of it.* **Volume 7 therefore spends the Hand once and refuses it once, and the closing position is one use and one refusal with the first still running.**
- **The closing position is the one the ending needs.** A man the reader has seen in a room, in his own frame, **three times** — ch 272 in Volume 5, ch 338 in Volume 6, ch 401 in Volume 7 — **and there is no fourth such scene in ch 361–420.** He is coherent, addressable, findable, legible to something that has his name on a sheet, **and answerable**, which is a liability rather than an asset and is the correct thing for `outline/ending.md` to inherit.

**What Volume 7 costs him that is not a state he had before.** Not a limb, not a hold, not a count of breath, not the unnamed state — all carried. **It is that he is now a thing that can be quoted, and he chose it, and he cannot un-choose it, and the woman whose foundation paid for it has given up a want she carried for two volumes to keep the price down.** *And if he does it, I will not know. That is the price and I am not going to be able to ask anybody about it, and there is no answer to that from anywhere on nine days of road* — said by her, about him, at ch 400, to a man who has already agreed to do it.

**What may not happen, and did not.** This close has not re-framed ch 361–420, has not re-titled the volume, has not introduced a new final enemy, and has taken nothing `outline/ending.md` owns. Grep across the volume returns **zero** for `Scribe`, `Anchor`, `Sisterhood`, `bargain`, `Feng`, `Lung`, `appointment` and bare `Order`. **The year of non-forgiveness is not retired, he is not forgiven, and she never tells him to stop — the subject is not mentioned once in sixty chapters**, and Volume 7's allowance for mentioning it is zero. **No figure of what is left of him is stated on any page in ch 361–420**, and the settlements at ch 370, 396 and 414 are shares out of *her* on listed work, not quantities of him. `Ledger` returns **0**. **And the ending still needs him findable, and he is.**

### The Volume 8 hand-off, as facts on the page

**The question, from `outline/volume-07.md` §*New question for Volume 8*, unchanged and not previewed here:** *what happens to the first person who comes for the reader.*

**And, measured at ch 420 and not derived from furniture:**

- **She is at NASCENT SOUL 7**, taken at ch 420 on day 1,076, the thirteenth of the month beginning 1,064, at the fourth hour in front of nine men on a lifting floor, after a dressed stone went the length of her own arm and stopped. **Bracket 6 of 6.** Stage cost: **the reach will not go out of a room she has not been asked into.**
- **She can never be paid.** A column carrying a person past nine has no last day on it and no figure against it, so no office on nine days of road can enter her hours as wages. **The bare board was a season. This is the rest of the run.** The board at the works rail is full, twenty-nine lines, every one measured by the weight or the length, and she can take neither
- **Six costs stand, each named once and none re-derived:** the furnace will not light itself · the hand cannot write a false line · a thing she reaches cannot be put back · she cannot be given anything · she cannot be paid · the reach answers a question and only a question
- **She has given up knowing what the fine hand says about her, forever, on purpose**, and the floor's response is to treat her as somebody who may know something, four times a month, with nothing written at the end of it
- **The reach is now something that has to be pointed.** It went once in two hours on a lifting floor and it went when a man of thirty asked her for it, and **the last line of the volume is that the only thing she could do about any of it was be asked**
- **The last irreversible decision is hers and it was made in a room on an ordinary condition and cost her everything she wanted and nothing anybody could take off her, and nothing failed**
- **Still open, and Volume 8 inherits all of it live:** the Hunter's request pending and the line's own people still not knowing she exists · everything above the offices that vouched, unread and unnamed, **and no office named anywhere in sixty chapters** · the fine hand and the man who can read it, both spent and neither given back · *kiln-born* in two heads and on no paper · what became of the eleven · the register still *reconciled* and **not read once in Volume 7** · the plate still worn and **not described once** · the year still carrying and **not mentioned once** · **the relicensed licence still never opened** · MEMBER ABSENT standing three times and not raised · Hou Sanliang paid and never met · Ning Bo's account discharged · Ning Wu's decade a line with no mark on it · and **the strip that went east on the end-of-month cart and did not come back, and the column that came back with two lines off it, and a stair book going up a road once a month with a name in it in two books that shut**
- **Volume 7 hands Volume 8 its antagonist with not one word of the Order on its page.** What Volume 7 actually puts on the page is a strip that went east eleven miles and a room four miles up a made road with a lamp in it and a man of forty-three who has read the fine hand for twenty-six years and offered it once. **The thing that sent the strips has stopped needing her to be near anything, and the last one did not come back on a cart at all. There is a country that big and something in it has been sending for her by name since before she knew she was being read.** That is a consequence of ch 402, ch 417 and ch 420 and not a fresh piece of furniture, and the audit confirms nothing of `outline/ending.md` is previewed

**This phase wrote no chapter file, no `workspace/volume-08-*` directory, no `workspace/volume-07-close-record/`, no `.done` marker and no second prompt.**

## Volume 7 close repair order — the sixteen findings, and the phase that must clear them

**Written by the Volume 7 close *review* fix pass, which repaired no chapter and changed no planned plot.** The
close audit recorded sixteen findings and repaired none, correctly, because a close that repairs is a rewrite.
**That left the debt with nobody holding it: `PHASE_SYSTEM.md` gives the close-record phase one job, which is to
create the next volume's planning phase, and on that rule Volume 8 planning would have been built on top of a
manuscript carrying six named weekdays, a volume number on the page, two outline headings, a stray token and three
live contradictions.** This section exists to stop that. **It is the obligation, not a note about one.**

**THE ORDER IS FIXED: this repair phase runs before Volume 8 planning, and Volume 8 planning does not open until
it has.** A Volume 8 planning phase that reads this file will find seven chapter files to reopen and is expected to
have them clean. **Nothing here re-opens a volume's plot, a card beat, a day, a stage, a title, a bracket or the
planned ending, and nothing here may.**

| # | Finding | File and line | The fix, and the one thing not to do |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| F1 | A named weekday, six times, in a world that has none | `chapter-0391.md:19`, `chapter-0395.md:69,93,101`, `chapter-0399.md:101`, `chapter-0400.md:117` | Substitute the month-day the chapter already supplies. **Do not edit `bible/world.md`** — its ch 1–238 claim is scoped and still true, and its rule was broken by the page, not by the canon |
| F2 | `Volume 6` in body prose | `chapter-0417.md:135` | Replace with the in-world name of the materials entry. **It is the only listing of that entry in Volume 7, so carry a date — do not just cut the two words** |
| F3 | Outline card headings on the page | `chapter-0409.md:43` `## Mouth Five` · `chapter-0415.md:63` `## Mouth Six` | Recast to what the section does in the world's terms, as the Batch 0003 review did at ch 401 and ch 404. **Keep the two sections where they are; only the two headings move** |
| F4 | A stray token in the last scene | `chapter-0420.md:135` **“Nai.”** | One word, in Geng's mouth, at the beat where the floor takes her column. **Right.** / **All right.** / **Yes.** are established within a page. Fix the word, not the beat |
| F5 | The Hunter's position stated three ways | `chapter-0369.md:59` two hundred yards west · `chapter-0404.md:117` and `chapter-0420.md:163` eleven miles east · `chapter-0418.md:79` made road east of the turn | Pick one and make the other three agree with it. **Eleven miles east is the far edge past the turn, where a man with a chain is turned back four hundred times (ch 366:79), and two chapters describe him as never moving** — the figure most likely to be wrong is the one that puts him at the far edge |
| F6 | One walk measured two ways | `chapter-0378.md` — heading `## The Mile And A Half Up The Road`, body *two miles* ×3, close *a mile and a half* | One distance, five instances. **Do not touch the section heading's position in the chapter or the burn on the man's hand** |
| F7 | An unresolvable referent on his cost | `chapter-0401.md:87` and `:91` | *what happened the one time* currently points at two different events, one of which is ch 378 and cannot be it. Name the one meant. **This is the volume's pivot chapter and the protagonist's third state is being paid for in it** |
| F8–F13 | Six recorded figures that do not reproduce | `state/continuity.md` rows for Batch 0002 and Batch 0004 | Already re-derived and corrected in the close's own tables at `## Volume 7 close — the volume as a whole`. **Verify, do not re-derive, and do not reopen the chapters they describe** |
| F14 | A false claim in the outline | `outline/volume-07.md` — *Six new people, none of them one of Volume 6's seven* | Five are new. Ch 409's man with the brush is Volume 6's ch 331 man. **The page is sound and the outline's claim about it is not. `outline/volume-07.md` is not to be re-planned by a repair phase — record it and leave the file alone** |

**WHAT MUST NOT BE TOUCHED WHILE CLEARING ANY OF THIS.** The volume's own answer at ch 417 is hers — six words in
a room, on an ordinary condition, and nothing failed — and **no finding above weakens it and no finding above
reaches it.** The fourteen fixed items of `outline/volume-07.md` all PASS and stay passing: the goal sentence
answered in Rao's mouth at ch 390, the six Nascent Soul days, **six bracketed layer lines in sixty and all six
spent**, the six costs each named once, the three shapes, the six mouths, Yuan's track, the three settlements, the
pressure rotation, the titles. **The planned plot, the planned ending and the Volume 8 question are untouched by
this section and are not reopened by it.**

**WHERE THE SAME THING IS SAID.** `workspace/volume-07-close/SUMMARY.md` carries the single-file version for any
phase that does not want to read five state files, and `state/continuity.md` carries the figures at
`## Volume 7 close — the volume as a whole`.

## Volume 7 planned — `outline/volume-07.md` exists and what the plan fixed

**Recorded by the Volume 7 planning phase. It is a planning phase: it wrote `outline/volume-07.md` and
`workspace/volume-07-batch-0001/PROMPT.md` and wrote no chapter prose, no second prompt and no `.done` marker,
which `PHASE_SYSTEM.md` gives to the controller alone. `outline/series.md` and `outline/ending.md` were not opened
and are unchanged.**

**Goal, title and range, all fixed by `outline/series.md` §9 and none re-planned here:** *Stronger Things*, ch
361–420, sixty chapters — *In this volume, Ning Yan must survive being the weakest person in every room she walks
into, or she learns that strength was never what she was taken for.*

### The pressure, in one sentence

**She is read correctly and understood wrongly.** From ch 358 the offices read her where she stands; the rooms east
of the mill are larger than any room she has stood in and full of people who were never inside a bound; **every one
of them takes her column, her furnace and her holder for granted, prices her at what she can lift, and she cannot
lift anything in them.** No villain in the volume, and the volume's worst things happen to her at the hands of
people who are right. **Its paper is the standing answer of Volume 6 — a refusal that cannot be taken out of a
book she cannot reach, a book of hers on a stone under a ladder, and a reach that happened once — being read by
rooms larger than any room she has stood in.**

### The day table, re-derived, not wished

**Month-day is `((day − 134) mod 30) + 1`; day 134 was the first of a month; months are thirty days.** The volume
runs **day 849 to day 1,090** — 242 days over sixty chapters, against Volume 6's 228 days over sixty-two. Months in
Volume 7 begin on days 854 · 884 · 914 · 944 · 974 · 1,004 · 1,034 · 1,064.

| Batch | Chapters | Days | Month-days at the ends |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0001 | 361–374 (14) | 849–899 | the 26th of the month beginning 824 → the 16th of the month beginning 884 |
| 0002 | 375–389 (15) | 900–950 | the 17th of the month beginning 884 → the 7th of the month beginning 944 |
| 0003 | 390–404 (15) | 951–1,020 | the 8th of the month beginning 944 → the 17th of the month beginning 1,004 |
| 0004 | 405–420 (16) | 1,021–1,090 | the 18th of the month beginning 1,004 → the 27th of the month beginning 1,064 |

**Batch 0003 ends on a pinned stage day (day 999) and Batch 0004 opens twenty-two days later. The chapters carry
that stretch as elapsed, exactly as Volume 6's Batch 0003 carried days 736–748, and the season turns inside it.**

### The power progression, derived before anything else

**She is at Nascent Soul 1 at ch 360.** `outline/series.md` §4 puts Nascent Soul 1–9 in Volumes 6–9, so **Volume 7
carries Nascent Soul 2 through 7 — six stages — and Volume 8 carries 8 and 9 and opens the Order.** Nothing is
moved out of the series table and no gap is left for a drafting phase to invent.

| Stage | Chapter | Day | Month-day | Interval | Cost, named once |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| NS 2 | 371 | 886 | the third of the month beginning 884 | **41**, the longest in the volume, and the chapter says why: the board was bare, the season had not turned, and a stage needs material she could not pay for | **the furnace will not light itself and she cannot light it alone** — a stage cannot be built by one pair of hands |
| NS 3 | 383 | 924 | the eleventh of the month beginning 914 | 38 | **her hand stops being able to lie** — a line she puts in ink in her own hand is a thing she means; she can still say a false thing out loud; she can no longer enter a false line in any book on that ground and there is no hand she can pay to enter one for her |
| NS 4 | 394 | 960 | the seventeenth of the month beginning 944 | 36 | **a thing she reaches cannot be put back where it was** |
| NS 5 | 404 | 999 | the twenty-sixth of the month beginning 974 | 39 | **she cannot be given anything** — a gift enters the column and the column can be read |
| NS 6 | 412 | 1,037 | the fourth of the month beginning 1,034 | 38 | **she cannot be paid** — a column carrying a person past nine has no last day and no figure against it, so no office on that ground can enter her hours as wages. **The bare board was a season; this is the rest of the run** |
| NS 7 | 420 | 1,076 | the thirteenth of the month beginning 1,064 | 39 | **the reach will not go out of a room she has not been asked into** — the last free thing she had is gone, and it is taken after she has won the room |

**The realm's series-table cost — *she can no longer be surprised by a person* — was already spent at ch 360 and is
not re-spent, re-explained or softened anywhere in Volume 7. Six new costs, each named once, each a different
thing. None is a limb, a hold, a count of breath or a missing finger, so none collides with the body's standing
injuries. **Three of the six are not losses at all** — three doors closing rather than three things taken away —
and two of those three do not touch her body in any way. None undoes a Volume 6 closure — the furnace is
not un-finished, it simply stops lighting itself, which is a different fact and a worse one.**

**Bracket budget: six in sixty — ch 371, 383, 394, 404, 412 and 420 — one at each Nascent Soul stage, in the far-side
house form character for character, and nowhere else.** Volume 6 spent six in sixty-two and none at Nascent Soul 1
because the handover was entered by hand. **The form does not change at all six. Ch 394 is where she hears the last
four words of it and finds they have stopped being true — there is no county within nine days of road — and she
thinks it, and the layer does not change.**

### The announcement rule, re-derived

**The rule did not change. The listeners did.** The layer still announces and the sound still goes into the ground,
because there is nobody inside a bound to be told and no county book to take it. **Volume 7's change is that the
rooms have learned to look.** A stage used to reach a strip three days late, in a fine hand, at a gate post; **from
ch 371 it happens in daylight in front of two or three people on that ground**, and after that it is discussed in
rooms she is not in, at figures she cannot check. **And she is entered as a body and not only as a column, which
is the reason she is the weakest thing in every room she walks into.** A person who can read a column out loud in a
room is not a clerk and not a cultivator and is on no board on that ground, and the offices cannot produce one,
because a column is read by whoever has been doing it for sixteen years and there is a post for that and there are
not two.

### The protagonist — the second rung of *Breaking*, stated so no drafter goes looking

`outline/series.md` §5 gives *Breaking* (Volumes 6–8) as opening the Wall and refusing the Order. **The Wall is the
Volume 6–10 structural answer and the Order is a Volume 8 antagonist; neither is opened here.** Volume 6 took the
first rung — it broke a rule about books (**no book on that reach shuts clean**, ch 344) and refused an act (**she
will not carry a holder into a room**, ch 358).

**Volume 7 takes the second rung, and it is the direction of a sending.** At ch 402 the holding is used, by him, on
purpose, for the first time, to move an office: an office asks for the holder, the holder answers, and the answer
goes back up a road and comes down her own road as a question about her. **What breaks is the assumption that a
line on this ground can only be read downward. What the offices can send for, can now be sent to.** It costs three
things, all named before they are spent: out of her foundation, told before spent; **the third frame scene away
from her hearing, at ch 401**; and **she can never again refuse a reading, because a second refusal would be a
refusal of an answer already given.**

**Yes, Volume 7 takes a third frame scene, and it is pinned at ch 401.** He has been on the page that way exactly
twice in six volumes — ch 272 (which made him findable) and ch 338 (which made him sendable) — and the third makes
him **answerable**, which is a third thing and worse than being sendable, because an answer can be quoted.
**Hand is spent once in Volume 7 (ch 402) and refused once, out loud, at ch 419.** Merit settles three times, each
on a listed piece of work cleared and not on a death, and nothing in Volume 7 kills anybody. **No figure of what
is left of him is stated on any page.**

### Two irreversible decisions, both hers, in different hands

1. **Ch 388, a yes and not a fight: she says a person's name out loud in a room and asks for something.** The first
   time since the Hunter, five months back on a cart track in wet grass, that she has asked anybody for anything.
   **It is entered in a room that keeps things and it cannot be unsaid.**
2. **Ch 417, and it is the volume's own: she declines to know what the fine hand says about her.** A man whose job
   it is will read it to her, once, in a room, on the ordinary condition that she confirms or denies it. **She says
   no. A denial is an entry.** Nothing is written anywhere, no book fails to shut, no circuit breaks, and she goes
   on not knowing, forever, on purpose. **Volume 6's break was a circuit broken, Volume 6's refusal was a permanent
   entry in a building she cannot reach, and Volume 7's refusal is an answer withheld: three acts, three shapes,
   none a restatement of another.**

### The four pressures, no two consecutive, none of the forbidden list

**0001 rooms bigger than she is** (361–374) · **0002 what she is worth in a room** (375–389) · **0003 the reach on
purpose** (390–404) · **0004 what it costs to be right** (405–420). **None is a tending, a ration, a hiring, a
crossing, an audit, an examination, a hunt, a signature, a keeping, a debt, a family paper, an ash or a
confinement, and none is Volume 6's *work without hire*.**

**Batch 0003 owns the break and Batch 0004 owns the refusal, and neither restates the other's as its own.** Each
batch carries at least one scene of physical risk or a real deadline, and each carries an irreversible step.

### The midpoint (ch 390) and the volume climax (ch 417–420)

**Midpoint:** Rao, who has kept the columns on the third floor for sixteen years, holds no furnace, cannot lift,
and reads Ning Yan's column back to her in the ordinary voice in a room — **and the column contains a refusal, and
it is the only refusal in the set, and nobody on that floor has ever had one to read.** The reversal is not a fact
about her strength: it is that what she has been trying to be paid for and what the rooms price her at are two
different things. **She was taken for her column, and a column's one talent is that it can refuse.** *The strongest
thing in this building is a column* — once, in her mouth, at ch 390, and nowhere else in the volume.

**Climax:** the refusal at ch 417 · the bill at ch 418 · **the Hand refused once at ch 419** · the lifting floor at
ch 419–420, a bar, a chain, a man under a dressed stone, a bell that has already gone, **and a stone moved about the
length of her own arm that cannot be put back** — **and ch 420, Nascent Soul 7 and a column read out loud in the
biggest room she has stood in, where she is the smallest thing in it.**

### What the structural answer does in Volume 7

**Six mouths, six new people, none of them one of Volume 6's seven, four or five sentences each, never a speech:**
a carter (ch 365) · a gate man (ch 379) · **Rao** (ch 391) · a woman who drives clay (ch 398) · a copying man below
Rao (ch 409) · **Geng**, who runs the lifting floor (ch 415). **The tenth part is still not given and nothing proves
the crews wrong**, the origin answer is Volume 11's and the moral answer is Volumes 15–16's, and the surface answer
was paid once at ch 267 and is not re-voiced.

### Three new named people, and one that stays unnamed

**Rao**, who keeps the columns on the third floor, sixteen years, and is the volume's reversal. **Dai**, whose job
it is to read the fine hand, who offers once and is refused once and is right both times. **Geng**, who runs the
lifting floor, who is strong, who is correct, and whose floor is a block short for a week because of what she did
to it. **The keeper with the paste pot stays unnamed and gets the heaviest scene in the volume**, and **the Hunter
is on the page exactly twice, once in Batch 0001 and once in Batch 0004, and she asks him both times.**

### What is still Volume 8's, and was left unspent here

`outline/series.md` §6 item 5 gives the Order of the Recruited to Volume 8 and **not one word of it is on Volume 7's
page.** The new question hands it over anyway: **the last strip did not come back on the cart at all, she has never
been east of here, and something in a country that big has been sending for her by name since before she knew she
was being read.** The question Volume 7 opens onto it: *what happens to the first person who comes for the reader.*

## Volume 6 close audit — OPENED

**`outline/volume-06.md` walked line by line against ch 299–360 as it stands after the Batch 0004 review repair and
after the close repairs below. The table is the audit; every row is a named item in the outline with a page
reference. `outline/series.md` and `outline/ending.md` were not opened. No chapter prose was written in this
phase except the close repairs recorded in `## Volume 6 close audit — CLOSED` and in `state/continuity.md`
under *Volume 6 close — repairs*.**

### 1. The one-sentence goal, fixed by `outline/series.md` §9

> *In this volume, Ning Yan must survive what is on the other side, or the crossing was a death and not an escape.*

**PAID, and paid in the chapters and not in a plan.** Every element the card names is on the page:

| What the card names | Page | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The far side is ordinary farmed country with offices in it and none of them is an enemy | ch 300–304, and no card creates a villain anywhere in the volume | **PASS** |
| The pressure is that being legible has a price list and she is the one paying it | ch 308 (*Seven Coppers At The Gate*), ch 350 (*What The Quiet Is For*, the anger with no target), ch 353 (*The Weight Goes To The Bar*) | **PASS** |
| She is entered twice and has found out the second vouching was copied upward in a hand she has never seen | entered once at ch 304 (*What The Counter Said*); second vouching agreed at ch 329 (*She Asked At The Gate*); **the finding at ch 357 (*Copied Upward In A Fine Hand*)** | **PASS** |
| She refused a sending out loud and it is entered and standing in a book she cannot reach | **ch 358 (*She Says No At The Counter*)** — refused in daylight, her name said, entered in the seasonal book with the twenty-sixth against it, in a building she cannot reach | **PASS** |
| She broke the closed-season box circuit and the bill is a book of hers on a stone under a keeper's ladder at a post | broken at **ch 344 (*The Board Is For That*)**; the bill at **ch 359 (*A Book Under A Ladder*)** | **PASS** |
| She reached Nascent Soul 1 by hand and not by sound, on a bank, where an office wrote it down afterward because the hand can read what happened | **ch 360 (*The Length Of A Reach*), day 845, no bracket** | **PASS** |

**The cost of surviving is stated in the chapters and not in a plan**, in four places: the heavy end closed for good
(ch 352, *It Takes Two Hands Now*); **the gate down from a third of a reach and one and a half coppers to a fifth
and one copper** (ch 353, *The Weight Goes To The Bar*); **a bare board at the works and a yard that goes quiet
until the season turns** (ch 356, *The Room With Two Things In It*); **a refusal she cannot take back** (ch 358);
**and a reach that happened once** (ch 360). No cost is left implicit at the last page.

ramp was never hers.* **PASS.**
